```tsx
// app/uploader.tsx
"use client";
import { Dropzone, httpTransport, useUpload } from "@hiplip/hatid/react";
import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from "react";
import type { AppUploads } from "@/lib/hatid";
import type { FileRecord } from "@/lib/store";

const transport = httpTransport<AppUploads>("/api/upload");

export function Uploader() {
  const [files, setFiles] = useState<FileRecord[]>([]);
  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {
    const res = await fetch("/api/files");
    setFiles(res.ok ? ((await res.json()) as FileRecord[]) : []);
  }, []);
  useEffect(() => { void refresh(); }, [refresh]);

  const { upload, items, progress, status, retry, cancel, reset } = useUpload({
    transport, route: "document", maxFiles: 5, onAllComplete: () => void refresh(),
  });

  const remove = async (key: string) => {
    await fetch("/api/files/delete", { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify({ key }) });
    await refresh();
  };

  return (
    <main style={{ maxWidth: 720, margin: "40px auto", padding: "0 16px" }}>
      <h1>hatid example</h1>
      <p><strong>Demo session only:</strong> your identity is a random cookie.</p>

      <Dropzone upload={upload} accept={["image/*", "application/pdf", "text/plain"]} maxFiles={5}>
        {({ getRootProps, getInputProps, isDragActive }) => (
          <div {...getRootProps({ style: { border: "2px dashed #888", borderRadius: 8, padding: 32, textAlign: "center", cursor: "pointer", background: isDragActive ? "#eef3ff" : undefined } })}>
            <input {...getInputProps()} />
            Drop files here or click to choose (images, PDF, text; max 5 MB each)
          </div>
        )}
      </Dropzone>

      <p>
        <button type="button" onClick={() => void upload(new File([new Uint8Array(6 * 1024 * 1024)], "too-big.pdf", { type: "application/pdf" }))}>
          Try a 6 MB file (rejected by the server)
        </button>{" "}
        <button type="button" onClick={reset}>Clear finished</button>
      </p>

      <p>Overall: {Math.round(progress * 100)}% ({status})</p>
      <ul>
        {items.map((item) => (
          <li key={item.id}>
            {item.fileName ?? "(blob)"}: {item.status} {Math.round(item.progress * 100)}%
            {item.status === "uploading" && <button type="button" onClick={() => cancel(item.id)}>Cancel</button>}
            {item.error && <> ({item.error.code}: {item.error.message}){item.error.retryable && <button type="button" onClick={() => void retry(item.id)}>Retry</button>}</>}
          </li>
        ))}
      </ul>

      <h2>Your files</h2>
      <ul>
        {files.map((f) => (
          <li key={f.key}>
            {f.fileName ?? f.key} ({f.size.toLocaleString()} bytes){" "}
            <a href={`/api/files/download?key=${encodeURIComponent(f.key)}`}>Download</a>{" "}
            <button type="button" onClick={() => void remove(f.key)}>Delete</button>
          </li>
        ))}
      </ul>
    </main>
  );
}
```

2. `cp examples/next/.env.example examples/next/.env` and fill it in.
3. Configure the bucket CORS for `http://localhost:3000` (see the root README).
4. `pnpm --filter hatid-example-next dev`.
5. A checklist to try: upload with progress, refresh and list, download, delete, and the 6 MB button showing `FILE_TOO_LARGE`.

### Task 21: `examples/cleanup-worker` (Workers cron)

**Files:** `examples/cleanup-worker/package.json`, `wrangler.toml`, `tsconfig.json`, `src/index.ts`, `README.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `cleanupUnconfirmed`, `createR2Client`.
- Produces: an hourly cron Worker. It doubles as proof that `/server` bundles for Workers with no Node built-ins.

- [ ] **Step 1: Files**

```json
{
  "name": "hatid-cleanup-worker",
  "private": true,
  "type": "module",
  "scripts": { "check": "wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir dist", "deploy": "wrangler deploy" },
  "dependencies": { "@hiplip/hatid": "workspace:*" }
}
```

Run: `pnpm --filter hatid-cleanup-worker add -D wrangler @cloudflare/workers-types typescript`

```toml
# wrangler.toml
name = "hatid-cleanup"
main = "src/index.ts"
compatibility_date = "2026-09-01"

[triggers]
crons = ["0 * * * *"] # hourly

[vars]
R2_PRIVATE_BUCKET = "hatid-dev"
# Secrets (never commit): wrangler secret put R2_ACCOUNT_ID / R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID / R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY
```

```ts
// src/index.ts
import { cleanupUnconfirmed, createR2Client } from "@hiplip/hatid/server";

type Env = { R2_ACCOUNT_ID: string; R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID: string; R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: string; R2_PRIVATE_BUCKET: string };

export default {
  async scheduled(_controller, env) {
    const r2 = createR2Client({
      accountId: env.R2_ACCOUNT_ID,
      accessKeyId: env.R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID,
      secretAccessKey: env.R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY,
      buckets: { private: env.R2_PRIVATE_BUCKET },
    });
    // Free plan: 50 subrequests per invocation. On paid plans raise `limit` (e.g. 900).
    const result = await cleanupUnconfirmed(r2, { olderThan: "24h", receiptTtl: "7d", limit: 45 });
    console.log(JSON.stringify({ hatidCleanup: result }));
  },
} satisfies ExportedHandler<Env>;
```

`tsconfig.json`: `{ "compilerOptions": { "target": "ES2022", "module": "ESNext", "moduleResolution": "Bundler", "strict": true, "types": ["@cloudflare/workers-types"], "noEmit": true }, "include": ["src"] }`

`README.md` covers:
- deploy steps (secrets, `wrangler deploy`);
- that `done: false` simply continues next hour;
- the lifecycle-rule alternative.

### Task 22: README

**Files:**
- Create: `README.md` (replace if present)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the verification results (`docs/specs/2026-10-08-r2-verification.md`) and the final API.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the README with exactly these sections**

1. **hatid**: one-paragraph pitch ("hatid" is Filipino for "to deliver"). It's a library, not a service: no dashboard, API keys, billing or multi-tenancy. It's built on Cloudflare R2 (10 GB free, free egress) and works with any S3-compatible endpoint, though only R2 is tested.
2. **How it works**: the issue → PUT → confirm diagram from spec §1, plus the safety rules from spec §2.10 as a bullet list.
3. **Install**: `npm i @hiplip/hatid` (peers: `react` for `/react`, `next` for `/next`, `@trpc/server` + `@trpc/client` for `/trpc`).
4. **R2 setup**:
   - Create two buckets (e.g. `myapp-private`, `myapp-public`). The private bucket must **never** have a custom domain or r2.dev access enabled. The public bucket gets a custom domain (R2 → bucket → Settings → Custom Domains).
   - Create an R2 API token with **Object Read & Write** scoped to those buckets. Record the Account ID, Access Key ID and Secret Access Key.
   - CORS: paste this into **both** buckets (R2 → Settings → CORS policy), edit origins, and use the V8 result to pick between the wildcard and the explicit list:
     ```json
     [
       {
         "AllowedOrigins": ["https://your.app", "http://localhost:3000"],
         "AllowedMethods": ["PUT", "GET", "HEAD"],
         "AllowedHeaders": ["content-type", "x-amz-meta-hatid-v", "x-amz-meta-hatid-route", "x-amz-meta-hatid-owner",
           "x-amz-meta-hatid-visibility", "x-amz-meta-hatid-size", "x-amz-meta-hatid-max-size", "x-amz-meta-hatid-type",
           "x-amz-meta-hatid-issued-at", "x-amz-meta-hatid-meta"],
         "ExposeHeaders": ["ETag"],
         "MaxAgeSeconds": 3600
       }
     ]
     ```
     Without these headers, browser uploads fail CORS. Without `ETag` exposed, multipart uploads fail with a clear error.
   - **Lifecycle rules** (optional backstop): delete objects with prefix `pending/` after 1 day and `receipts/` after 7 days, and abort incomplete multipart uploads after 1 day.
5. **Define routes**: the `createR2Client` + `defineUploads` example from spec §2.1, including `input`, `auth`, `rateLimit` and `onConfirmed`. Rules to call out:
   - `auth` is required.
   - `onConfirmed` is at-least-once, so upsert on `key`.
   - `input` and `metadata` are **plaintext object metadata**: identifiers only, never secrets, PII, file names or content.
   - `maxSize` units are 1024-based.
   - `allowedTypes` can't be `*/*`. Avoid `image/svg+xml` on public routes unless your public domain is isolated from your app (SVG can carry script).
6. **Next.js (Vercel)**: `app/api/upload/route.ts` with `export const { POST } = createNextHandler(uploads)`, plus the client `useUpload` + `<Dropzone>` snippet with `httpTransport<typeof uploads>("/api/upload")`.
7. **Cloudflare Workers**: `createFetchHandler(uploads)` inside a Worker `fetch`, credentials from `env` secrets, and a note that `createR2Client` must be created per request or lazily from `env`.
8. **tRPC**: `defineUploads.withContext<Context>()`, `createUploadRouter({ t, procedure, uploads })`, `trpcTransport(trpcClient.upload)`.
9. **Downloads and deletes**: `createDownloadUrl` (with `downloadName`), `publicUrl`, `deleteFile`/`headFile`, with a warning that these do **no** authorization, so check ownership in your DB first.
10. **Encrypt before upload**: pass an `ArrayBuffer`/`Uint8Array`, which uploads as `application/octet-stream`. Use `datePrefix: false`, keep file names out of keys, and send `fileName` only if it's already encrypted (base64url).
11. **Rate limiting**:
    - Cloudflare Workers Rate Limiting binding (`[[unsafe.bindings]] type = "ratelimit"`, then `env.UPLOAD_LIMITER.limit({ key: owner })`).
    - Upstash for Vercel: `new Ratelimit({ redis: Redis.fromEnv(), limiter: Ratelimit.slidingWindow(20, "1 m") })`, returning `success || { retryAfter }` from `rateLimit`. Show both as short code blocks.
12. **Cleanup**: `cleanupUnconfirmed` and the `examples/cleanup-worker` cron, plus the lifecycle-rule alternative.
13. **Errors**: the code table from spec §2.9 plus the client-only codes `TOO_MANY_FILES`, `NETWORK` and `CANCELED`.
14. **What hatid is not**: no dashboard, API keys, image transforms or hosted service (yet). `StorageBackend` is `@experimental` until 1.0.
15. **Contributing / testing**: `pnpm test` (fake R2 with real signature checks). `pnpm test:integration` runs against a **dev** bucket only, because it sweeps `pending/`.

Every code block must compile against the final API. Copy from the example app and tests rather than writing from memory.

### Task 23: CI and release with npm trusted publishing

**Files:**
- Create: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `scripts/publish.mjs`, `.changeset/initial-release.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: CI on PRs/pushes; a release flow where changesets opens a version PR, and merging it publishes via OIDC.

- [ ] **Step 1: `ci.yml`**

```yaml
name: CI
on:
  push: { branches: [main] }
  pull_request:
permissions: { contents: read }
jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy: { matrix: { node: [20, 22] } }
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: "${{ matrix.node }}", cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm typecheck
      - run: pnpm test
      - run: pnpm build
      - run: pnpm lint:pkg
      - run: pnpm --filter hatid-cleanup-worker check
  integration:
    if: github.event_name == 'push'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: 22, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm test:integration # skips itself when the secrets are absent
        env:
          R2_ACCOUNT_ID: ${{ secrets.R2_ACCOUNT_ID }}
          R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID: ${{ secrets.R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID }}
          R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: ${{ secrets.R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY }}
          R2_PRIVATE_BUCKET: ${{ secrets.R2_PRIVATE_BUCKET }}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: `scripts/publish.mjs`**

```js
// Publishes the root package with npm (OIDC trusted publishing) if this version isn't on npm yet.
import { execSync } from "node:child_process";
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";

const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(new URL("../package.json", import.meta.url), "utf8"));
const spec = `${pkg.name}@${pkg.version}`;
let published = false;
try {
  published = execSync(`npm view ${spec} version`, { stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "ignore"] }).toString().trim() === pkg.version;
} catch { /* not found */ }
if (published) {
  console.log(`${spec} is already published; nothing to do.`);
  process.exit(0);
}
execSync("pnpm build", { stdio: "inherit" });
execSync("npm publish --access public --provenance", { stdio: "inherit" });
execSync(`git tag v${pkg.version}`, { stdio: "inherit" });
console.log(`New tag: ${pkg.name}@${pkg.version}`);
```

This uses `npm publish` directly rather than `changeset publish`, which runs `pnpm publish` under pnpm. npm ≥ 11.5.1 is the documented path for trusted publishing; switch to `changeset publish` only if pnpm's OIDC support is verified. The final `New tag:` line lets `changesets/action` create the GitHub release.

- [ ] **Step 3: `release.yml`**

```yaml
name: Release
on:
  push: { branches: [main] }
concurrency: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}
permissions:
  contents: write       # version PR + tags
  pull-requests: write  # version PR
  id-token: write       # npm trusted publishing (OIDC)
jobs:
  release:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: 22, cache: pnpm, registry-url: "https://registry.npmjs.org" }
      - run: npm install -g npm@latest # trusted publishing needs npm >= 11.5.1
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm test
      - uses: changesets/action@v1
        with:
          version: pnpm changeset version
          publish: pnpm release
          createGithubReleases: true
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
```

No `NPM_TOKEN` and no OTP: npm authenticates the workflow through OIDC. Provenance is attached automatically.

### Task 24: Follow-up plans and final verification

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/plans/2026-10-08-followups.md`
- Modify: `docs/specs/2026-10-08-hatid-v1-design.md` (implementation notes)

- [ ] **Step 1: Write `docs/plans/2026-10-08-followups.md`**

```markdown
# hatid follow-ups (after v0.1.0 is published)

## 1. Hiplip/saas-starter (Next.js, tRPC, Drizzle, Better Auth, Neon)

1. `pnpm add @hiplip/hatid`. Env (`.env.example` + the env schema): `R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`,
   `R2_PRIVATE_BUCKET`, optional `R2_PUBLIC_BUCKET` + `R2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`.
2. Drizzle table `files`:
   `id uuid pk default random`, `key text not null unique`, `ownerId text not null references user(id) on delete cascade`,
   `size bigint not null`, `contentType text not null`, `visibility text not null`, `fileName text`, `createdAt timestamptz default now()`.
   Index on (`ownerId`, `createdAt`). Generate and run the migration.
3. `server/uploads.ts`: `createR2Client` from env, then `defineUploads.withContext<TRPCContext>()` with an `avatar` (public, image/*, 2MB)
   and an `attachment` (private) route. `auth: ({ ctx }) => ctx.session?.user.id ?? null`.
   `onConfirmed` → `db.insert(files).values(…).onConflictDoNothing({ target: files.key })`.
4. tRPC: `upload: createUploadRouter({ t, procedure: publicProcedure, uploads })` in the app router (auth lives in the routes).
   Add `files.list` (owner-scoped), `files.downloadUrl` (owner check, then `createDownloadUrl`), and `files.delete` (owner check, then `deleteFile` + row delete).
5. Client: `useUpload({ transport: trpcTransport<typeof uploads>(trpcClient.upload), route: "attachment" })` + `<Dropzone>` wrapped in shadcn/ui.
6. Rate limit with Upstash (`@upstash/ratelimit`), keyed by owner.
7. Cleanup: add an R2 lifecycle rule (no Workers in this template) and document `cleanupUnconfirmed` for a Vercel Cron route as an option.
8. Tests: owner scoping on list/download/delete; onConfirmed idempotency (double confirm → one row).

## 2. Hiplip/nimbus (end-to-end encrypted attachments)

Constraint: nothing plaintext server-side. Ciphertext only; no file names in keys or metadata.

1. Route `attachment`: `visibility: "private"`, `prefix: "att"`, `datePrefix: false`, no `keyExtension`/`keyFileName`,
   `allowedTypes: ["application/octet-stream"]`, `maxSize` sized for ciphertext overhead (plaintext limit + AEAD tag/header).
2. `input: z.object({ noteId: z.string().uuid() })`. This is an identifier only, and `auth` checks the user can write to that note.
3. Client: encrypt with the note key (e.g. AES-GCM via WebCrypto), then `upload({ data: ciphertext, type: "application/octet-stream", fileName: encryptedNameB64url })`.
   The encrypted name must be base64url (survives hatid's display-name cleaning) and ≤ 1024 chars.
4. `onConfirmed` stores `{ key, noteId, size, encryptedName }` (size is ciphertext size). It never stores the plaintext name or type;
   the real MIME type goes inside the encrypted envelope.
5. Download: server checks note access, then `createDownloadUrl({ key, expiresIn: "1m" })` with no `downloadName`. The client fetches,
   decrypts, and builds a Blob with the decrypted name and type.
6. Deletion: deleting a note deletes its attachment rows, then `deleteFile` for each key (or a queue for large notes).
7. Threat-model notes: R2 sees ciphertext sizes and timing; `owner` and `noteId` sit in object metadata (identifiers, acceptable).
   Consider padding ciphertext to size buckets if size leakage matters.
```
